# Craft-cards provenance map — Python corpus

Out-of-runtime maintainer record. Neither consumer receives this file: a card carries the rule and its numbers. A card's line here is the sole record of its sources: every citation with its URL, fetch status and quoted statements, the formulation the rule follows, and the notes, including `caveat: …` on the evidence's scope. A card carries none of it and is found by its `rule_id`. Round-trip rule: a card plus its line here plus its topic's research note reconstruct everything known about the rule.

One line per card: `rule_id · evidence citation(s), each with its URL, its fetch status and its quoted statements · topic (research note) · formulation source · reception · notes` (evidence strength, ownership, `caveat: …` on the evidence's scope).

Fetch status per citation, as the design note's source policy defines it: `fetched` — opened from the authoring environment and quoted; **relayed** — the search index's statement of the claim, quoted, the paper itself unopened; **unfetched** — cited from memory, never a card's only anchor. A relayed or unfetched citation is openable by any reader and stands to be re-verified.

---

- **PY-127** · SonarSource python rule S6890 (zoneinfo should be preferred to pytz when using Python 3.9 and later), §Why is this an issue?, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonarSource/sonar-python/master/python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S6890.html — "In Python 3.9 and later, the zoneinfo module is the recommended tool for handling timezones, replacing the pytz library. … You can directly use zoneinfo timezone objects when creating datetime objects, making it more intuitive and less error-prone than pytz, which requires a separate localize method for this purpose. … When a pytz timezone object is used, it defaults to the earliest known offset, which can lead to unexpected results. zoneinfo does not have this issue."; SonarSource python rule S6887 (pytz.timezone should not be passed to the datetime.datetime constructor), §Why is this an issue? - **PY-41** · PEP 8 §Imports (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/peps/main/peps/pep-0008.rst) — "should be avoided, as they make it unclear which names are present in the namespace, confusing both readers and many automated tools."; PEP 8 §Imports (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/peps/main/peps/pep-0008.rst) — "There is one defensible use case for a wildcard import, which is to republish an internal interface as part of a public API (for example, overwriting a pure Python implementation of an interface with the definitions from an optional accelerator module and exactly which definitions will be overwritten isn't known in advance)."; PEP 8 §Imports (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/peps/main/peps/pep-0008.rst) — "When republishing names this way, the guidelines below regarding public and internal interfaces still apply."; CPython Doc/reference/simple_stmts.rst §The import statement (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/reference/simple_stmts.rst) — "The names given in ``__all__`` are all considered public and are required to exist."; - **PY-169** · Ruff TID255 lazy-import-immediately-resolved (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_tidy_imports/rules/lazy_import_immediately_resolved.rs, Why is this bad?, Known problems, Fix safety) — 'When a lazily imported name is used immediately at module load time, the import is resolved eagerly, defeating the purpose of marking the import as lazy. This is commonly caused by using a lazily imported module in a module global or in a top-level class definition.', '`@dataclasses.dataclass` can immediately resolve lazy imports used in field annotations without triggering this rule.' and 'converting a lazy import to an eager import changes when the imported module is executed, which can change runtime behavior if the module has import-time side effects.'; PEP 810 §Semantics — 'The module is not loaded immediately at the import statement; instead, a lazy proxy object is created and bound to the name. The actual module is loaded on first use of that name.'; §Do lazy imports work with circular imports? — 'making the imports lazy might help **only if** the circular reference isn’t accessed during module initialization. However, if either module accesses the other during import time, you’ll still get an error.'; §What about forwards compatibility with older Python versions? — 'On Python versions before 3.15 that don’t support lazy imports, the `__lazy_modules__` attribute is simply ignored and imports proceed eagerly as normal.' · Ruff TID255 lazy-import-immediately-resolved (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_tidy_imports/rules/lazy_import_immediately_resolved.rs) — (fetched) · Ruff TID255 lazy-import-immediately-resolved (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_tidy_imports/rules/lazy_import_immediately_resolved.rs) — 'The rule also recognizes imports made lazy by a literal `__lazy_modules__` declaration, even when the target version is older than Python 3.15.' ## ERR — errors and resilience
- **PY-11** · Ruff B904 raise-without-from-inside-except, §Why is this bad? (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_bugbear/rules/raise_without_from_inside_except.rs; cache src/ruff/rules.json) — "When raising a new exception from within an `except` clause, it's recommended to include a `from` clause to explicitly set the exception's cause. Without it, Python will implicitly chain from the current exception (setting `__context__`), but the `__cause__` attribute won't be set, which may make debugging slightly more difficult."; flake8-bugbear README.rst (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PyCQA/flake8-bugbear/main/README.rst; cache src/bugbear/README.rst) §B904 — "Within an ``except`` clause, raise exceptions with ``raise ... from err`` or ``raise ... from None`` to distinguish them from errors in exception handling."; Pylint raise-missing-from (W0707), message description (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pylint-dev/pylint/main/doc/data/messages/r/raise-missing-from/bad.py; cache items.jsonl) — "When you raise a new exception after another exception was caught it's likely that the second exception is a friendly re- wrapping of the first exception. In such cases `raise from` provides a better link between the two tracebacks in the final error."; CPython Doc/tutorial/errors.rst §Raising Exceptions, §Exception Chaining (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/tutorial/errors.rst) — "a simpler form of the :keyword:`raise` statement allows you to re-raise the exception … If an unhandled exception occurs inside an :keyword:`except` section, it will have the exception being handled attached to it and included in the error message … To indicate that an exception is a direct consequence of another, the :keyword:`raise` statement allows an optional :keyword:`from<raise>` clause … It also allows disabling automatic exception chaining using the ``from None`` idiom"; PEP 8 §Programming Recommendations (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/peps/main/peps/pep-0008.rst) — "Use exception chaining appropriately. raise X from Y should be used to indicate explicit replacement without losing the original traceback. When deliberately replacing an inner exception (using raise X from None), ensure that relevant details are transferred to the new exception" · Ruff B904 raise-without-from-inside-except (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_bugbear/rules/raise_without_from_inside_except.rs) — 'Checks for `raise` statements in exception handlers that lack a `from` clause. ... When raising a new exception from within an `except` clause, it's recommended to include a `from` clause to explicitly set the exception's cause. Without it, Python will implicitly chain from the current exception (setting `__context__`), but the `__cause__` attribute won't be set, which may make debugging slightly more difficult.' (fetched) · flake8-bugbear B904 (PyCQA/flake8-bugbear README.rst) — (fetched) · Pylint raise-missing-from (W0707), message description (pylint-dev/pylint pylint/checkers/exceptions.py, W0707; harvest items.jsonl) — (fetched) · CPython Doc/tutorial/errors.rst §Raising Exceptions, §Exception Chaining (python/cpython Doc/tutorial/errors.rst) — 'a simpler form of the :keyword:`raise` statement allows you to re-raise the exception ... If an unhandled exception occurs inside an :keyword:`except` section, it will have the exception being handled attached to it and included in the error message ... During handling of the above exception, another exception occurred ... To indicate that an exception is a direct consequence of another, the :keyword:`raise` statement allows an optional :keyword:`from<raise>` clause ... The above exception was the direct cause of the following exception ... It also allows disabling automatic exception chaining using the ``from None`` idiom' (fetched) · PEP 8 §Programming Recommendations (python/peps peps/pep-0008.rst) — 'Use exception chaining appropriately. ``raise X from Y`` should be used to indicate explicit replacement without losing the original traceback. When deliberately replacing an inner exception (using ``raise X from None``), ensure that relevant details are transferred to the new exception (such as preserving the attribute name when converting KeyError to AttributeError, or embedding the text of the original exception in the new exception message).' (fetched) · topic: python-exceptions-and-context-managers (research/software-craft-python/python-exceptions-and-context-managers.md) · formulation: none · reception: not contested · notes: confidence high; all five sources state the implicit __context__ chain and the from err / from None forms, and the tutorial prints both traceback separators the card quotes; Ruff files B904 as pedantic and rates the cost as debugging "slightly more difficult", hence severity minor; the card does not carry the except* wording of Ruff's message, and its Limits on bare raise and raise of the caught name rest on Ruff's "raising a new exception" scope and the tutorial's re-raise form.
- **PY-12** · PEP 8 §Programming Recommendations (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/peps/main/peps/pep-0008.rst) — "A bare except: clause will catch SystemExit and KeyboardInterrupt exceptions, making it harder to interrupt a program with Control-C, and can disguise other problems. If you want to catch all exceptions that signal program errors, use except Exception: … A good rule of thumb is to limit use of bare … If the exception handler will be printing out or logging the traceback; at least the user will be aware that an error has occurred. … If the code needs to do some cleanup work, but then lets the exception propagate upwards with"; CPython documentation library/exceptions §SystemExit, §KeyboardInterrupt (https://docs.python.org/3/library/exceptions.html) — "It inherits from BaseException instead of Exception so that it is not accidentally caught by code that catches Exception. This allows the exception to properly propagate up and cause the interpreter to exit. … The exception inherits from BaseException so as to not be accidentally caught by code that catches Exception and thus prevent the interpreter from exiting."; Ruff E722 bare-except (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/pycodestyle/rules/bare_except.rs; cache src/ruff/rules.json) — "A bare `except` catches `BaseException` which includes `KeyboardInterrupt`, `SystemExit`, `Exception`, and others. Catching `BaseException` can make it hard to interrupt the program (e.g., with Ctrl-C) and can disguise other problems."; Pylint bare-except (W0702) details.rst (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pylint-dev/pylint/main/doc/data/messages/b/bare-except/details.rst; cache src/pylint/bare-except/details.rst) — "A good rule of thumb is to limit use of bare ‘except’ clauses to two cases: - If the exception handler will be printing out or logging the traceback; at least the user will be aware that an error has occurred. - If the code needs to do some cleanup work, but then lets the exception propagate upwards with raise."; flake8-bugbear README.rst (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PyCQA/flake8-bugbear/main/README.rst; cache src/bugbear/README.rst) §B001 — "Do not use bare except:, it also catches unexpected events like memory errors, interrupts, system exit, and so on. Prefer except Exception:. If you're sure what you're doing, be explicit and write except BaseException:."; flake8-bugbear README.rst (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PyCQA/flake8-bugbear/main/README.rst; cache src/bugbear/README.rst) §B036 — "Found except BaseException: without re-raising (no raise in the top-level of the except block). This catches all kinds of things (Exception, SystemExit, KeyboardInterrupt...) and may prevent a program from exiting as expected."; SonarSource python S5754 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonarSource/sonar-python/master/python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S5754.html; cache src/sonar/S5754.html) — "It is recommended to catch instead a more specific exception. If it is not possible, the exception should be raised again."; Ruff BLE001 blind-except (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_blind_except/rules/blind_except.rs; cache src/ruff/rules.json) — "Overly broad `except` clauses can lead to unexpected behavior, such as catching `KeyboardInterrupt` or `SystemExit` exceptions that prevent the user from exiting the program. … Exceptions that are logged with `exc_info` enabled will _not_ be flagged" · PEP 8 §Programming Recommendations (python/peps peps/pep-0008.rst) — 'A bare ``except:`` clause will catch SystemExit and KeyboardInterrupt exceptions, making it harder to interrupt a program with Control-C, and can disguise other problems. If you want to catch all exceptions that signal program errors, use ``except Exception:`` (bare except is equivalent to ``except BaseException:``). A good rule of thumb is to limit use of bare 'except' clauses to two cases: 1. If the exception handler will be printing out or logging the traceback; at least the user will be aware that an error has occurred. 2. If the code needs to do some cleanup work, but then lets the exception propagate upwards with ``raise``. ``try...finally`` can be a better way to handle this case.' (fetched) · CPython documentation library/exceptions §SystemExit, §KeyboardInterrupt (https://docs.python.org/3/library/exceptions.html) — 'This exception is raised by the sys.exit() function. It inherits from BaseException instead of Exception so that it is not accidentally caught by code that catches Exception. This allows the exception to properly propagate up and cause the interpreter to exit. ... Raised when the user hits the interrupt key ... The exception inherits from BaseException so as to not be accidentally caught by code that catches Exception and thus prevent the interpreter from exiting.' (fetched) · Ruff E722 bare-except (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/pycodestyle/rules/bare_except.rs) — (fetched) · Pylint bare-except (W0702) (pylint-dev/pylint doc/data/messages/b/bare-except/details.rst) — (fetched) · flake8-bugbear B001 (PyCQA/flake8-bugbear README.rst) — (fetched) · flake8-bugbear B036 (PyCQA/flake8-bugbear README.rst) — (fetched) · SonarSource python S5754 (SonarSource/sonar-python python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S5754.html) — 'It should, however, be raised again to allow the interpreter to exit as expected. ... (fetched) · Ruff BLE001 blind-except (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_blind_except/rules/blind_except.rs) — 'Overly broad `except` clauses can lead to unexpected behavior, such as catching `KeyboardInterrupt` or `SystemExit` exceptions that prevent the user from exiting the program. ... Exceptions that are re-raised will _not_ be flagged ... Exceptions that are logged with `exc_info` enabled will _not_ be flagged' (fetched) · topic: python-exceptions-and-context-managers (research/software-craft-python/python-exceptions-and-context-managers.md) · formulation: none · reception: contested — tolerant: PEP 8 and Pylint W0702 details (and Ruff BLE001, which does not flag a broad handler that re-raises or logs with exc_info) accept a catch-all that prints or logs the traceback or cleans up and re-raises; strict: Sonar S5754 and bugbear B036 require the re-raise in every case; never-bare: Ruff E722, Pylint W0702 and bugbear B001 report every bare except; separating condition: the handler prints or logs the traceback — the card follows PEP 8 there · notes: confidence high on the mechanism (PEP 8, the CPython exceptions reference), moderate on the tolerated logging case, which the strict side rejects; the Validator counts a top-level sys.exit() call as a re-raise because sys.exit() raises SystemExit, which extends B036's "raise in the top level"; GeneratorExit, which also derives from BaseException, is not in the triggers; whether a catch-all belongs at a given place is not carried here.
- **PY-13** · CPython documentation reference/compound_stmts §except clause (https://docs.python.org/3/reference/compound_stmts.html) — "For an except clause with an expression, the expression must evaluate to an exception type or a tuple of exception types. Parentheses can be dropped if multiple exception types are provided and the as clause is not used. … If no except clause matches the exception, the search for an exception handler continues in the surrounding code and on the invocation stack." and the 3.14 version note "Support for optionally dropping grouping parentheses when using multiple exception types."; Ruff PLW0711 binary-op-exception (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/pylint/rules/binary_op_exception.rs; cache src/ruff/rules.json) — "A binary operation will not catch multiple exceptions. Instead, the binary operation will be evaluated first, and the result of _that_ operation will be caught (for an `or` operation, this is typically the first exception in the list). This is almost never the desired behavior."; Pylint binary-op-exception (W0711), message description (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pylint-dev/pylint/main/doc/data/messages/b/binary-op-exception/bad.py; cache items.jsonl) — "Used when the exception to catch is of the form" (the message names except A or B: and asks for except (A, B): when several types are meant); SonarSource python S5714 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonarSource/sonar-python/master/python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S5714.html; cache src/sonar/S5714.html) — "The result of a boolean expression of exceptions is a single exception class, thus using a boolean expression in an except block will result in catching only one kind of exception." · CPython documentation reference/compound_stmts §try statement, §except clause (https://docs.python.org/3/reference/compound_stmts.html) — 'Changed in version 3.14: Support for optionally dropping grouping parentheses when using multiple exception types. ... For an except clause with an expression, the expression must evaluate to an exception type or a tuple of exception types. Parentheses can be dropped if multiple exception types are provided and the as clause is not used. ... If no except clause matches the exception, the search for an exception handler continues in the surrounding code and on the invocation stack.' (fetched) · Ruff PLW0711 binary-op-exception (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/pylint/rules/binary_op_exception.rs) — (fetched) · Pylint binary-op-exception (W0711), message description (pylint-dev/pylint pylint/checkers/exceptions.py, W0711; harvest items.jsonl) — 'Used when the exception to catch is of the form "except A or B:". - **PY-14** · CPython documentation reference/compound_stmts §except clause (https://docs.python.org/3/reference/compound_stmts.html) — "This search inspects the except clauses in turn until one is found that matches the exception. … The raised exception matches an except clause whose expression evaluates to the class or a non-virtual base class of the exception object, or to a tuple that contains such a class."; SonarSource python S1045 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonarSource/sonar-python/master/python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S1045.html; cache src/sonar/S1045.html) — "If there is a handler for a base class followed by a handler for class derived from that base class, the second handler will never trigger: The handler for the base class will match the derived class, and will be the only executed handler. When multiple except statements try to catch the same exception class, only the first one will be executed."; flake8-bugbear README.rst (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PyCQA/flake8-bugbear/main/README.rst; cache src/bugbear/README.rst) §B047 — "Unreachable except handler. (fetched) · SonarSource python S1045 (SonarSource/sonar-python python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S1045.html) — 'This rule raises an issue when an except statement has had all its exceptions caught by a previous except clause. ... an except block is dead code as it will never catch any exception ... - **PY-88** · CPython Doc/library/asyncio-task.rst §Task cancellation (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/library/asyncio-task.rst), fetched: "It is recommended that coroutines use ``try/finally`` blocks to robustly perform clean-up logic. - **PY-170** · flake8-bugbear B042, README.rst §List of warnings (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PyCQA/flake8-bugbear/main/README.rst), fetched: "Exception classes with a custom `__init__` should pass all args to `super().__init__()` to work correctly with `copy.copy` and `pickle`. Both `BaseException.__reduce__` and `BaseException.__str__` rely on the `args` attribute being set correctly, which is set in `BaseException.__new__` and `BaseException.__init__`. If you define `__init__` yourself without passing all arguments to `super().__init__` it is very easy to break pickling, especially if they pass keyword arguments which both `BaseException.__new__` and `BaseException.__init__` ignore. It's also important that `__init__` not accept any keyword-only parameters. Alternately you can define both `__str__` and `__reduce__` to bypass the need for correct handling of `args`. If you define `__str__/__reduce__` in super classes this check is unable to detect it, and we advise disabling it."; CPython Doc/builtins/exceptions.rst §Base classes, attribute BaseException.args (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/builtins/exceptions.rst), fetched: "The tuple of arguments given to the exception constructor." · flake8-bugbear B042 (PyCQA/flake8-bugbear README.rst) — (fetched) · flake8-bugbear B042 (PyCQA/flake8-bugbear README.rst) — (fetched) · flake8-bugbear B042 (PyCQA/flake8-bugbear README.rst) — (fetched) · flake8-bugbear B042 (PyCQA/flake8-bugbear README.rst) — (fetched) · flake8-bugbear B042 (PyCQA/flake8-bugbear README.rst) — (fetched) · flake8-bugbear B042 (PyCQA/flake8-bugbear README.rst) — (fetched) · CPython Doc/builtins/exceptions.rst §Base classes, BaseException.args (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/builtins/exceptions.rst) — (fetched) · topic: python-exceptions-and-context-managers (research/software-craft-python/python-exceptions-and-context-managers.md) · formulation: none · reception: not contested · notes: confidence high; B042 is on by default in flake8-bugbear (harvest index); I reproduced the failure on Python 3.14.7 with the Example's bad class (pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(e)) and copy.copy(e) both raise TypeError for the missing 'source') and the good class round-trips; the card carries the exemption for __str__ plus __reduce__ on the class or a superclass, not the tool's advice to disable the check, which concerns the tool only.

- **PY-174** · Ruff RUF030 assert-with-print-message, assert_with_print_message.rs ## Why is this bad? (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/ruff/rules/assert_with_print_message.rs), fetched: "If an `assert x, y` assertion fails, the Python interpreter raises an `AssertionError`, and the evaluated value of `y` is used as the contents of that assertion error. The `print` function always returns `None`, however, so the evaluated value of a call to `print` will always be `None`. Using a `print` call in this context will therefore output the message to `stdout`, before raising an empty `AssertionError(None)`. Instead, remove the `print` and pass the message directly as the second expression, allowing `stderr` to capture the message in a well-formatted context."; CPython Doc/reference/simple_stmts.rst §The assert statement (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/reference/simple_stmts.rst), fetched: "The extended form, ``assert expression1, expression2``, is equivalent to :: if __debug__: if not expression1: raise AssertionError(expression2)" · Ruff RUF030 assert-with-print-message (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/ruff/rules/assert_with_print_message.rs) — (fetched) · Ruff RUF030 assert-with-print-message (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/ruff/rules/assert_with_print_message.rs) — (fetched) · Ruff RUF030 assert-with-print-message (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/ruff/rules/assert_with_print_message.rs) — (fetched) · Ruff RUF030 assert-with-print-message (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/ruff/rules/assert_with_print_message.rs) — (fetched) · Ruff RUF030 assert-with-print-message (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/ruff/rules/assert_with_print_message.rs) — (fetched) · Ruff RUF030 assert-with-print-message (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/ruff/rules/assert_with_print_message.rs) — (fetched) · CPython Doc/reference/simple_stmts.rst §The assert statement (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/reference/simple_stmts.rst) — (fetched) · CPython Doc/reference/simple_stmts.rst §The assert statement (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/reference/simple_stmts.rst) — (fetched) · topic: python-exceptions-and-context-managers (research/software-craft-python/python-exceptions-and-context-managers.md) · formulation: none · reception: not contested · notes: confidence high; ## PRF — diagnostics and performance
- **PY-51** · Ruff PLE1141 dict-iter-missing-items, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/pylint/rules/dict_iter_missing_items.rs (harvest cache src/ruff/rules.json), §Why is this bad? and §Known problems: "When iterating over a dictionary in a for loop, if a dictionary is unpacked without calling `.items()`, it could lead to a runtime error if the keys are not a tuple of two elements." and "The tuple key is unpacked into `x` and `y` instead of the key and values." (preview rule); SonarSource python S7517, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonarSource/sonar-python/master/python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S7517.html, §Why is this an issue?: "the v variable would attempt to unpack the key itself, which can lead to errors or subtle bugs if the key is iterable, like a string."; Pylint dict-iter-missing-items (E1141), https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pylint-dev/pylint/main/doc/data/messages/d/dict-iter-missing-items/bad.py: "for city, population in data:  # [dict-iter-missing-items]"; CPython Doc/builtins/stdtypes.rst §Mapping Types, iter(d), https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/builtins/stdtypes.rst: "Return an iterator over the keys of the dictionary. ## INP — input and security hygiene
- **PY-95** · Ruff S108 hardcoded-temp-file, Why is this bad? (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_bandit/rules/hardcoded_tmp_directory.rs): 'The use of hardcoded paths for temporary files can be insecure. If an attacker discovers the location of a hardcoded path, they can replace the contents of the file or directory with a malicious payload.'; Ruff S306 suspicious-mktemp-usage, Why is this bad? (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_bandit/rules/suspicious_function_call.rs): '`tempfile.mktemp` returns a pathname of a file that does not exist at the time the call is made; then, the caller is responsible for creating the file and subsequently using it. This is insecure because another process could create a file with the same name between the time the function returns and the time the caller creates the file.'; Bandit B108 hardcoded_tmp_directory, plugin docstring (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PyCQA/bandit/main/bandit/plugins/general_hardcoded_tmp.py): 'This plugin test looks for strings starting with (configurable) commonly used temporary paths' and 'Any string starting with one of these fragments will report a MEDIUM confidence issue.'; Bandit B306 mktemp_q, blacklist calls (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PyCQA/bandit/main/bandit/blacklists/calls.py): 'Use of insecure and deprecated function (mktemp).'; SonarSource python S5443, Why is this an issue? and How to fix it in Python Standard Library (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonarSource/sonar-python/master/python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S5443.html): 'When an application creates files in these directories with predictable names, it becomes vulnerable to race conditions: an attacker can create a file with the same name before the application does, potentially causing the application to read or write attacker-controlled content.' and the compliant 'tempfile.TemporaryFile(dir="/tmp/my_subdirectory", mode="w+")'; SonarSource python S5445, Why is this an issue? and Strong security controls (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonarSource/sonar-python/master/python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S5445.html): 'Creating temporary files in a non-atomic way introduces race condition issues in the application’s behavior. Indeed, a third party can create a given file between when the application chooses its name and when it creates it.' and 'Temporary files can be created using unsafe functions and API as long as strong security controls are applied.'; CPython Doc/library/tempfile.rst §mkstemp, §mkdtemp, §mktemp, §TemporaryFile, §NamedTemporaryFile, §TemporaryDirectory and the module introduction (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/library/tempfile.rst): 'Creates a temporary file in the most secure manner possible.  There are no race conditions in the file's creation, assuming that the platform properly implements the os.O_EXCL flag for os.open.  The file is readable and writable only by the creating user ID.', 'deprecated:: 2.3 Use :func:`mkstemp` instead.' and 'Use of this function may introduce a security hole in your program.  By the time you get around to doing anything with the file name it returns, someone else may have beaten you to the punch.' · Ruff S306 suspicious-mktemp-usage (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_bandit/rules/suspicious_function_call.rs) — (fetched) · Ruff S108 hardcoded-temp-file (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_bandit/rules/hardcoded_tmp_directory.rs) — (fetched) · Bandit B108 (PyCQA/bandit doc/source/plugins/b108_hardcoded_tmp_directory.rst) — (fetched) · Bandit B306 (PyCQA/bandit doc/source/blacklists/blacklist_calls.rst) — (fetched) · SonarSource python S5443 (SonarSource/sonar-python python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S5443.html) — (fetched) · SonarSource python S5443 compliant solution (SonarSource/sonar-python python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S5443.html) — 'file = tempfile.TemporaryFile(dir="/tmp/my_subdirectory", mode="w+") # Compliant' (fetched) · SonarSource python S5445 (SonarSource/sonar-python python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S5445.html) — (fetched) · SonarSource python S5445 requirements (SonarSource/sonar-python python-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S5445.html) — 'They should be generated using a cryptographically secure random generator.' - **PY-96** · Pylint eval-used (W0123), message summary (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pylint-dev/pylint/main/doc/data/messages/e/eval-used/bad.py, message text from the harvest index): 'Used when you use the "eval" function, to discourage its usage. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_bandit/rules/suspicious_function_call.rs): 'The `eval()` function is insecure as it enables arbitrary code execution. If you need to evaluate an expression from a string, consider using `ast.literal_eval()` instead, which will raise an exception if the expression is not a valid Python literal.'; Bandit B307 eval, blacklist calls (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PyCQA/bandit/main/bandit/blacklists/calls.py): 'Use of possibly insecure function - consider using safer ast.literal_eval.'; CPython Doc/library/ast.rst §literal_eval (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/library/ast.rst): 'This function had been documented as "safe" in the past without defining what that meant. That was misleading. This is specifically designed not to execute Python code, unlike the more general eval. There is no namespace, no name lookups, or ability to call out. But it is not free from attack: A relatively small input can lead to memory exhaustion or to C stack exhaustion, crashing the process. There is also the possibility for excessive CPU consumption denial of service on some inputs. Calling it on untrusted data is thus not recommended.'; CPython Doc/library/functions §eval, warning (https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html): 'This function executes arbitrary code. Calling it with untrusted user-supplied input will lead to security vulnerabilities.' · Pylint eval-used (W0123) (pylint-dev/pylint pylint/checkers/base/basic_checker.py, W0123 message definition) — (fetched) · Ruff S307 suspicious-eval-usage (astral-sh/ruff crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_bandit/rules/suspicious_function_call.rs) — (fetched) · Bandit B307 (PyCQA/bandit doc/source/blacklists/blacklist_calls.rst) — (fetched) · CPython Doc/library/ast.rst §literal_eval (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/main/Doc/library/ast.rst) — 'This is specifically designed not to execute Python code, unlike the more general :func:`eval`.
